/// Run the whole-region ladder over every smooth region.
pub(super) fn classify_regions(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    regions: &[Region],
    config: &Config,
) -> Classification {
    let centroids: Vec<[f64; 3]> = mesh
        .faces
        .iter()
        .map(|f| face_centroid(f, &mesh.vertices))
        .collect();

    let mut cls = Classification::new();
    let mut ladder_wins = [0usize; 4]; // plane, sphere, conical, torus
    let mut fallback_regions = 0usize;
    for region in regions {
        if classify_one_region(mesh, region, &centroids, config, &mut cls, &mut ladder_wins) {
            continue;
        }
        // Terminal fallback: per-facet planes (total coverage can never fail).
        fallback_regions += 1;
        for &fi in &region.faces {
            cls.push_planar(facet_plane(mesh, fi));
        }
    }

    if config.verbosity >= 1 {
        eprintln!(
            "  ladder: {} plane, {} sphere, {} cyl/cone, {} torus; {} region(s) → per-facet fallback",
            ladder_wins[0], ladder_wins[1], ladder_wins[2], ladder_wins[3], fallback_regions,
        );
    }
    cls
}
```

**After:**
```
/// Run the whole-region ladder over every smooth region; regions the ladder
/// cannot explain go to scoped competition (2.3(b)), and whatever competition
/// leaves unclaimed degrades to per-facet planes.
pub(super) fn classify_regions(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    regions: &[Region],
    jet: &[JetField],
    config: &Config,
) -> Classification {
    let centroids: Vec<[f64; 3]> = mesh
        .faces
        .iter()
        .map(|f| face_centroid(f, &mesh.vertices))
        .collect();

    let mut cls = Classification::new();
    let mut ladder_wins = [0usize; 4]; // plane, sphere, conical, torus
    let mut competed_regions = 0usize;
    let mut competed_surfaces = 0usize;
    let mut facet_faces = 0usize;
    for region in regions {
        if classify_one_region(mesh, region, &centroids, config, &mut cls, &mut ladder_wins) {
            continue;
        }
        // The ladder cannot explain this region — scoped competition.
        competed_regions += 1;
        let (hyps, leftovers) = compete::compete_region(mesh, region, jet, &centroids, config);
        competed_surfaces += hyps.len();
        for hyp in hyps {
            cls.push_compete(hyp);
        }
        // Whatever competition left unclaimed → per-facet planes (total
        // coverage can never fail; a single triangle is exactly a plane).
        facet_faces += leftovers.len();
        for fi in leftovers {
            cls.push_planar(facet_plane(mesh, fi));
        }
    }

    if config.verbosity >= 1 {
        eprintln!(
            "  ladder: {} plane, {} sphere, {} cyl/cone, {} torus; competition: {} region(s) → {} surfaces + {} per-facet",
            ladder_wins[0], ladder_wins[1], ladder_wins[2], ladder_wins[3],
            competed_regions, competed_surfaces, facet_faces,
        );
    }
    cls
}
```

        };

        let debug = config.verbosity >= 3 || std::env::var("BREPPER_DEBUG_COMPETE").is_ok();

        // Plane is always a candidate (cheap; catches misread flats).
        let plane = grow::<PlanarSurface>(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet, centroids, config, 1);
        let mut cone_area = None;
        let mut sphere_area = None;
        if a1 >= flat_eps {
            // Curved: a developable (cylinder/cone) is always plausible.
            let cone = grow::<ConicalSurface>(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet, centroids, config, MIN_CYLINDER_FACES);
            cone_area = cone.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
            consider(cone.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Conical(h), a)), 2, &mut best);
            // Curved in both directions → also try a sphere.
            if a2 >= 0.3 * a1 {
                let sphere = grow::<SphericalSurface>(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet, centroids, config, MIN_SPHERE_FACES);
                sphere_area = sphere.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
                consider(sphere.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Spherical(h), a)), 1, &mut best);
            }
        }
        let plane_area = plane.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
        consider(plane.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Planar(h), a)), 0, &mut best);

        if debug && best.is_none() {
            eprintln!(
                "    compete: seed {seed} jet=({:.3},{:.3}) class {:?} → NO GROWTH (plane {plane_area:?} cone {cone_area:?} sphere {sphere_area:?})",
                jf.k1, jf.k2, curvature_class(jf),
            );
        }
```
</details>

/// Max faces in the jet-similar seed patch used to condition the initial fit.
const SEED_PATCH_CAP: usize = 48;

/// Refit-and-reflood rounds while growing one hypothesis.
const MAX_GROW_ROUNDS: usize = 8;

/// Flood-acceptance tolerance as a multiple of `--vertex-tolerance`. A fit
/// grown from partial support has ~1e-4 mm residual and a coarsely-tessellated

/// modest loosening bootstraps the grow without leaking across junctions.
/// Any residual one-row leak is corrected by claim resolution, and every
/// committed hypothesis is refit on its full support (accurate to ~1e-6 mm).
const GROW_VTOL_FACTOR: f64 = 30.0;
```

> The rounded cube's coarse mesh is detecting 8 corner spheres correctly, but the fillet cylinders aren't being found because the mesh is too coarse to fit them properly. The flats and fillets are being converted into 142 per-facet planes instead. When stage 3.4 generates the spherical and planar faces, BRepCheck is reporting invalid wires, which suggests there's a topology issue with how the faces are being constructed.
> 
> The problem is that the partial spheres are bounded by jagged per-facet plane boundaries, creating invalid wire geometry where they meet. Before adding competition, the all-per-facet approach produced a valid polyhedron, but now mixing spheres with per-facet planes creates bad boundaries at their interfaces. The real issue is that competition is finding spheres but missing the fillets, resulting in a worse decomposition than just using per-facet planes everywhere.
> 
> The cleanest fix would be ensuring that if competition can't fully decompose a tangent chain into analytic surfaces, the leftover per-facet regions don't create invalid boundaries against the analytic surfaces. But I'm noticing the flat faces also failed to decompose into multi-face planes on the coarse mesh — competition found 142 per-facet faces but zero multi-face planes, so the flat decomposition itself is broken on coarse.
> 
> Given the time constraints, I'm going to take a pragmatic approach: if a competed region ends up with too many per-facet leftovers (meaning competition mostly failed), I'll revert the whole region back to all-per-facet. This avoids the mixed-boundary BRepCheck failures while preserving the valid polyhedron fallback, though it does sacrifice the wins from the spheres that did decompose successfully. So fine's competition is solid with only a tiny leftover fraction, but coarse has a huge remainder that makes the mixed result unreliable. If I fall back to all-per-facet for coarse, I'm actually restoring the behavior that was passing before, which fixes the regression while keeping fine's competition improvement. I'll implement a check: after competition runs, if the leftover fraction exceeds a threshold, discard the competition results and use the per-facet approach instead.
> 
> This is heuristic but grounded — regions that are mostly leftover mean competition failed to find analytic structure, so treating them as freeform/per-facet makes sense. The tradeoff is losing the spheres in coarse to get a valid solid, since mixing analytic surfaces with jagged per-facet boundaries is inherently problematic for stage 3.
> 
> I'll apply the fallback: if a region's leftover fraction exceeds 40%, discard competition and use all-per-facet. This should keep competition for fine (where leftovers are ~0.06%) and fall back for coarse (where leftovers are ~70%). Now I need to verify the actual region face counts to confirm the leftover percentages.
> 
> For the implementation, I'll add a check in classify_regions after calling compete_region to compute the leftover fraction and decide whether to keep or discard the competition results. Setting the threshold at 50% means coarse will fall back while fine stays with competition.
> 
> Implementing the fallback logic...
> 
> The threshold makes sense: on coarse, competition correctly identified 8 spheres but left 74% of faces unclassified. Keeping both the spheres and per-facet classifications creates an invalid brep in stage 3, so falling back to pure per-facet is the safer choice for now—it restores the previous passing behavior, even though it loses the sphere detections. A future improvement could handle mixed classifications better or find the fillets on coarse.
> 
> Now I'm implementing the fallback logic and checking whether the mixed analytic plus per-facet approach actually breaks other test cases like nosecone or the fine rounded_cube variant. Nosecone has no leftovers so it's clean, but fine rounded_cube has 6 per-facet planes mixed with 26 analytic surfaces—I need to verify if that's what's causing the stage 4 failure or if it's a different model variant altogether.

        // The ladder cannot explain this region — scoped competition.
        competed_regions += 1;
        let (hyps, leftovers) = compete::compete_region(mesh, region, jet, &centroids, config);

        // If competition left most of the region unexplained, it did not
        // find real analytic structure — a handful of analytic surfaces
        // islanded in a sea of per-facet planes reconstructs to invalid
        // wires at their jagged mutual boundary (worse than a clean
        // polyhedron). Discard it and per-facet the whole region; freeform
        // fitting (stage 2.3(e)) is the principled home for such regions.
        if leftovers.len() * 2 > region.faces.len() {

        // coverage can never fail; a single triangle is exactly a plane).

## 🤖🔧 Assistant
<details>
<summary>Edit src/stage2/classify.rs</summary>

**Before:**
```
/// Sorted, deduplicated vertex indices of a face set.
fn vertices_of(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> Vec<usize> {
```

**After:**
```
/// Size of the largest mesh-connected component within a face set.
fn largest_component(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> usize {
    let member: HashSet<usize> = faces.iter().copied().collect();
    let mut seen: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
    let mut largest = 0usize;
    for &start in faces {
        if seen.contains(&start) {
            continue;
        }
        let mut size = 0usize;
        let mut stack = vec![start];
        seen.insert(start);
        while let Some(fi) = stack.pop() {
            size += 1;
            let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
            for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
                if ni >= 0 {
                    let ni = ni as usize;
                    if member.contains(&ni) && seen.insert(ni) {
                        stack.push(ni);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        largest = largest.max(size);
    }
    largest
}

/// Sorted, deduplicated vertex indices of a face set.
fn vertices_of(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> Vec<usize> {
```
</details>

This means the patch contains faces from both the cylinder and the cone, creating a compromise fit that's off by nearly a millimeter.
> 
> The solution is to make the seed_patch more selective so it doesn't cross the junction boundary. The key difference is that rounded_cube's fillet has sharp curvature transitions to the adjacent surfaces—the fillet itself is developable but the sphere and plane faces don't match its class, so they get excluded from the patch. With cone_cylinder though, both surfaces are developable and their principal curvatures align at the tangent junction, so the cylinder patch bleeds right into the cone instead of stopping cleanly.
> 
> I'm considering a few approaches: checking that the principal direction (the wrapping direction for cylinders and cones) stays consistent across the seed patch, or more practically, just using a much smaller seed patch—just the seed face plus its immediate neighbors—so it stays local and doesn't straddle the junction. The conditioning might be slightly worse, but then the flood-refit can grow it out properly from a clean foundation.
> 
> The real issue is that even with a small seed patch capped at something like 12 faces, it could still reach the junction if that's close enough. A better constraint would be to prevent the seed patch from crossing regions where the jet magnitude changes significantly—so from a cylinder seed where the curvature is low, I'd stop growing before reaching the cone where curvature increases.
> 
> But tightening the magnitude tolerance from 2× to 1.3× doesn't fully solve it either, since cone-junction faces still fall within that range. The actual fix might be to use a different initial fit strategy altogether, one that's robust enough to validate the seed before the flood-and-refit step.
> 
> Instead of starting with a large patch that straddles multiple surface types, I could seed the fit with just the face itself plus its immediate neighbors—a tight 1-ring or 2-ring of faces in the same class. This keeps the initial fit local and prevents the seed from being poisoned by distant geometry. The flood-fill can then grow outward from there, and the refit step will improve the overall fit as it expands. strategy: reduce the seed_patch to grow only 1–2 rings instead of the full cap, keeping it local and clean. I'm considering adding a depth limit to the BFS alongside the cap, so it stays around 13 faces. But I'm wondering if that's enough geometry for a fillet (which is a quarter-cylinder) to fit well.
> 
> The key insight is that jet-interior ordering should push the seed away from junctions—so even a 2-ring patch from a clean interior seed should stay away from cone-cylinder boundaries and remain a good fit.
> 
> I'm going to implement seed_patch with a BFS depth limit of 2 and a cap around 24 faces to keep patches manageable while staying locally coherent.

/// Max faces in the jet-similar seed patch used to condition the initial fit.

/// Max BFS depth of the seed patch. Kept shallow so the patch stays local to
/// the seed and does not bleed across a tangent junction between two surfaces
/// of the same curvature class (e.g. a cone meeting a cylinder, where the

<details>
<summary>Edit src/stage2/compete.rs</summary>

**Before:**
```
fn seed_patch(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, in_region: &[bool], seed: usize, jet: &[JetField]) -> Vec<usize> {
    let seed_class = curvature_class(jet[seed]);
    let seed_mag = jet[seed].k1.abs().max(jet[seed].k2.abs());
    let mut seen: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
    seen.insert(seed);
    let mut out = vec![seed];
    let mut stack = vec![seed];
    while let Some(fi) = stack.pop() {
        if out.len() >= SEED_PATCH_CAP {
            break;
        }
        let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
        for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
            if ni < 0 {
                continue;
            }
            let ni = ni as usize;
            if !in_region[ni] || seen.contains(&ni) {
                continue;
            }
            if curvature_class(jet[ni]) != seed_class {
                continue;
            }
            let mag = jet[ni].k1.abs().max(jet[ni].k2.abs());
            if seed_mag > 1e-9 && (mag > 2.0 * seed_mag || mag < 0.5 * seed_mag) {
                continue;
            }
            seen.insert(ni);
            out.push(ni);
            stack.push(ni);
        }
    }
    out.sort_unstable();
    out
}
```

**After:**
```
fn seed_patch(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, in_region: &[bool], seed: usize, jet: &[JetField]) -> Vec<usize> {
    let seed_class = curvature_class(jet[seed]);
    let seed_mag = jet[seed].k1.abs().max(jet[seed].k2.abs());
    let mut seen: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
    seen.insert(seed);
    let mut out = vec![seed];
    // Depth-limited BFS (frontier by ring).
    let mut frontier = vec![seed];
    for _depth in 0..MAX_PATCH_DEPTH {
        if out.len() >= SEED_PATCH_CAP {
            break;
        }
        let mut next = Vec::new();
        for &fi in &frontier {
            let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
            for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
                if ni < 0 {
                    continue;
                }
                let ni = ni as usize;
                if !in_region[ni] || seen.contains(&ni) {
                    continue;
                }
                if curvature_class(jet[ni]) != seed_class {
                    continue;
                }
                let mag = jet[ni].k1.abs().max(jet[ni].k2.abs());
                if seed_mag > 1e-9 && (mag > 1.5 * seed_mag || mag < 0.667 * seed_mag) {
                    continue;
                }
                seen.insert(ni);
                out.push(ni);
                next.push(ni);
                if out.len() >= SEED_PATCH_CAP {
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        frontier = next;
    }
    out.sort_unstable();
    out
}
```
</details>

<details>

```
//! Integration tests for stage 2.3(b) scoped fit competition: tangent-chain
//! regions the whole-region ladder cannot explain must decompose into their
//! true analytic surfaces (vertex-tolerance validated, not just counted).
//!
//! These run the full stage-2 pipeline (default stage) and assert the typed
//! hypothesis counts on the two headline acceptance models. Torus-requiring
//! chains (pipe elbow, toroidal fillets) are covered once stage 2.3(c)
//! promotion lands.

use brepper::config;
use brepper::{stage1, stage2};

fn manifest_dir() -> String {
    env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR").to_string()
}

fn run(stl_rel: &str) -> stage2::Stage2Output {
    let stl = format!("{}/{stl_rel}", manifest_dir());
    let config = config::parse_config_from(["brepper", &stl, "-q"]).unwrap();
    let mesh = stage1::stage1(&config).expect("stage1");
    stage2::stage2(&config, mesh, None).expect("stage2")
}

/// Every mesh face is covered by exactly one selected surface.
fn assert_total_coverage(out: &stage2::Stage2Output) {
    let n = out.mesh.faces.len();
    let mut count = vec![0u32; n];
    for s in &out.selected_surfaces {
        let faces = match s {
            stage2::SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => &out.planar_hypotheses[*i].faces,
            stage2::SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => &out.conical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
            stage2::SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => &out.spherical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
            stage2::SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => &out.toroidal_hypotheses[*i].faces,
            stage2::SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => &out.bspline_hypotheses[*i].faces,
        };
        for &fi in faces {
            count[fi] += 1;
        }
    }
    assert!(count.iter().all(|&c| c == 1), "every face covered exactly once");
}

#[test]
fn competition_rounded_cube_shell_decomposes_6_12_8() {
    // The rounded-cube outer shell is one smooth region (a tangent chain);
    // competition must split it into 6 planes + 12 fillet cylinders + 8
    // corner spheres — the reference's 26 analytic faces.
    let out = run("tests/onshape/rounded_cube_10_r2_fine.stl");
    let cylinders = out.conical_hypotheses.iter().filter(|h| h.is_cylinder()).count();
    let cones = out.conical_hypotheses.iter().filter(|h| !h.is_cylinder()).count();
    assert_eq!(out.spherical_hypotheses.len(), 8, "8 corner spheres");
    assert_eq!(cylinders, 12, "12 edge-fillet cylinders");
    assert_eq!(cones, 0, "no cones");
    assert_eq!(out.planar_hypotheses.len(), 6, "6 face planes");
    assert_eq!(out.selected_surfaces.len(), 26, "26 analytic surfaces total");
    assert_total_coverage(&out);
}

#[test]
fn competition_nosecone_cone_sphere_base() {
    // The nosecone's cone+sphere tangent chain is one region; the base disk
    // is a separate sharp-bounded plane. Competition + the ladder together
    // yield cone + sphere + base = 3 surfaces (the reference's 3 faces).
    let out = run("tests/onshape/nosecone_medium.stl");
    assert_eq!(out.spherical_hypotheses.len(), 1, "1 nose sphere");
    let cones = out.conical_hypotheses.iter().filter(|h| !h.is_cylinder()).count();
    assert_eq!(cones, 1, "1 body cone");
    assert_eq!(out.planar_hypotheses.len(), 1, "1 base plane");
    assert_eq!(out.selected_surfaces.len(), 3, "cone + sphere + base");
    assert_total_coverage(&out);
}

```
</details>

Seeds = region faces jet-interior-first (ascending `neighbor_jet_mismatch`); per seed grow each jet-pruned candidate (`grow::<S>`) and commit the LARGEST validated area (DOF tiebreak); claim resolution assigns multi-claimed junction faces to the best claimant (2× margin); leftovers → per-facet. `grow` = seed_patch (jet-similar, DEPTH-LIMITED BFS `MAX_PATCH_DEPTH=3`, cap 32, magnitude within 1.5×) → fit → `flood` (connected region faces validating the fit) → refit fixpoint. Without this, EVERYTHING returned NO GROWTH (seed failed its own fit at 5e-5). (2) **depth-limited seed patch** — a 48-face jet patch STRADDLED the cone↔cylinder tangent junction (both developable, k1 continuous across the seam) → fit 0.9mm off → seed fails; a shallow local patch stays on one surface (rounded_cube fillets never had this — they border spheres/planes = different curvature class, excluded). (3) **large-leftover-blob fallback** (`LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX=8` in classify.rs) — if competition leaves a connected leftover blob >8 faces (a whole sub-surface it couldn't fit: coarse fillet, or concave torus fillet awaiting step 5), DISCARD competition and per-facet the whole region → clean polyhedron, because an analytic surface islanded among per-facet planes reconstructs to INVALID wires at their jagged boundary (rounded_cube_coarse: 43 BRepCheck failures → 0). RESULTS: rounded_cube_10_r2_fine → 6 planar + 12 cyl + 8 sphere (=26, ref's 26), nosecone_medium → cone+sphere+base (=3, ref's 3); both build 1 valid solid at 0mm distance to reference (vol rel 4e-4 / 2e-3). Threaded `Segmentation.jet` into classify_regions. **Torus-needing models (cone_cylinder's concave fillet k2<0, filleted_*, pipe_elbow bend, stepped_block_toroidal) correctly fall back to valid per-facet — they need step 5 (bent-tube k=1/R promotion, compete.rs). pill's thin 1-ring cylinder wall is a separate hard case (fit ~0.011mm off). ```
Stage 2 rewrite: step 4 (scoped fit competition)

Implements DEVELOPMENT_PLAN Rewrite Roadmap step 4 — stage 2.3(b) scoped fit
competition (src/stage2/compete.rs). A smooth region the whole-region ladder
(2.3(a)) cannot explain is a tangent chain; competition decomposes it into
its true analytic surfaces, scoped inside the sharp-bounded region.

Algorithm:
- Seeds are region faces in jet-interior-first order (ascending curvature
  mismatch with neighbours); claimed faces never seed.
- Per seed, grow each jet-pruned candidate type (plane always; cylinder/cone
  when curved; sphere when curved in both directions) and commit the
  hypothesis with the largest validated area (simplest-DOF tiebreak). Growth
  is: a jet-similar seed patch → fit → flood-accept every region face the fit
  validates → refit on the grown support, to a fixpoint.
- A flood may grow through faces an earlier hypothesis already claimed
  (junction rows on two surfaces); resolve_claims assigns each contested face
  to the claimant fitting its vertices best (2x margin). Unclaimed faces are
  returned as leftovers (per-facet planes).

Three fixes the corpus forced:
- Flood accepts at 30x --vertex-tolerance (GROW_VTOL_FACTOR). A fit from
  partial support has ~1e-4 mm residual and a coarsely-tessellated flat reads
  ~1e-3 mm of plane residual — both far above the 5e-5 mm vertex tolerance yet
  far below the ~0.1 mm a neighbour diverges one row past a tangent junction.
  Every committed hypothesis is refit on full support (~1e-6 mm) and claim
  resolution corrects any one-row leak. Without this, every seed failed its
  own initial fit and nothing grew.
- The seed patch is depth-limited (MAX_PATCH_DEPTH) so it does not straddle a
  tangent junction between two same-class developables (a cone meeting a
  cylinder, where curvature is continuous across the seam) — a straddling
  patch fits neither.
- classify.rs discards competition and per-facets the whole region when it
  leaves a large connected leftover blob (LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX) — a whole
  sub-surface it could not fit. A few analytic surfaces islanded in per-facet
  planes reconstruct to invalid wires at their jagged boundary (rounded_cube
  coarse: 43 BRepCheck failures); a clean polyhedron is the safe fallback
  until freeform (2.3(e)) / torus (2.3(c)) give those blobs a home.

Results (full pipeline + --compare, valid solid at 0 mm distance to the
reference): rounded_cube_10_r2_fine -> 6 planes + 12 fillet cylinders + 8
corner spheres (26, the reference's 26); nosecone_medium -> cone + sphere +
base (3, the reference's 3). Sharp-edged models are unaffected (they pass the
ladder; 0 competition regions). New tests/stage2_competition.rs asserts both
decompositions and total coverage.

Torus-fillet chains (pipe_elbow bend, stepped_block_toroidal, cone_cylinder's
concave fillet, filleted_*) correctly fall back to a valid per-facet
polyhedron until bent-tube promotion (step 5) adds the torus. No regressions:
stage4 integration 25 -> 27 passing (nosecone_medium + rounded_cube now pass),
stage2/stage3 unchanged (130/99), lib 90 + segmentation 14 green.

---
Generated with assistance from Claude Opus 4.8 (Anthropic, 2026)

  step 4 and commit again." (this commit is the step-4 landing; the agent-log
  and steps 1-3 landed in the preceding commit)

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

```

## What to build

When a cylinder flood in competition **stalls at a bend** (a bent tube /
extrusion sweep: the straight pipe grows, then the bend faces sit
~R·Δφ²/2 ≫ tol off the cylinder fit and stop validating), read the stalled
frontier's jet. Tube-continuation signature (κ1 ≈ the claimed tube's 1/r, small
nonzero κ2) → attempt a torus promotion, seeded from a circle/line fit through
the claimed support's **medial points**. Incompatible signature ((0,0) plane,
(κ,κ) sphere) → stop, don't spend the refit.

Acceptance (for the DEBUG session, not this one): pipe_elbow → torus + 2
cylinders (no strip machinery); then stepped_block_toroidal, cone_cylinder's
concave fillet (jet k2<0), filleted_*. All currently fall back to a valid
per-facet polyhedron, so there is **no regression pressure** — a clean bar.

## Where it hooks into compete.rs (as I left it in step 4)

- `compete_region` seeds jet-interior-first and calls `grow::<S>` per candidate
  type, committing the largest validated area. `grow::<S>` = seed_patch → fit →
  `flood` (connected region faces validating the fit) → refit fixpoint, returns
  `(hyp, area, faces)`.
- The natural hook: after a `grow::<ConicalSurface>` **converges as a cylinder**
  (half_angle≈0) but its flood did NOT reach the whole developable run (there
  are unclaimed region-neighbours of the accepted set with tube-continuation
  jet), try to promote: grow a torus from the cylinder support ∪ bend frontier.
  Add it as a competing candidate for that seed (largest-area still decides), or
  as a dedicated promotion path — implementer's call.
- Multi-claim resolution, leftover→per-facet, and the `LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX`
  fallback in `classify.rs` already handle the rest; once the torus grows, the
  bend leftover vanishes and the region flows without touching that fallback.

## Reuse / scouting notes (save the implementer time)

- **Medial points are already on `JetField`** (`edges.rs`): `medial =
  centroid − n̂/k1` when `|k1|` clears the noise floor — the tube-center /
  curvature-center estimate. A circle/line fit through the claimed cylinder's
  medial points gives the spine (line for k=0, circle for the bend). This is
  the jet-informed spine seed the plan calls for; no pairwise normal-line
  intersection needed (the medial-axis torus fitter's `|n₁·n₂|>0.95` reject is
  exactly what strips trip — jet κ1 sidesteps it).
- **Try the cheap path first before writing new math**: pipe_elbow's bend is a
  *clean* torus, so once enough bend arc is claimed, the EXISTING
  `fit_torus`/`ToroidalSurface` (toroidal.rs, validates `0<r≤R`) may fit it
  directly — the hard part is *claiming* the bend faces, not the final fit. So
  the minimal viable step 5 might be: detect stall → seed a torus from medial
  points + a couple of bend rows → `flood::<ToroidalSurface>` → refit. Only if
  that fails on strip meshes do you need the full degenerate-limit `k=1/R`
  model with ridge-toward-0 regularization (the SOR/Dupin-cyclide direction in
  SURFACE_FITTING.md). Note the flood tolerance is 30×vtol (`GROW_VTOL_FACTOR`).
- **Model reduction at commit**: collapse `k≈0` (spine straight to within
  vertex tolerance over the claimed extent) back to a cylinder, so straight
  pipes stay cylinders.
- **Determinism** (hard-won): never iterate a HashSet/HashMap for fit sums —
  sort first; break ties by face index; the fitters recenter about the
  centroid (keep it).
- Debug tooling in place: `BREPPER_DEBUG_COMPETE=1` logs per-commit and
  NO-GROWTH seeds. Acceptance references: `tests/onshape/pipe_elbow_10_medium.stl`
  vs the reference STEP; `tests/onshape/stepped_block_toroidal_*`.

- Strip meshes (pipe_elbow ships full-length strips, NO interior vertices along

- The stall-detection heuristic (when has a cylinder "stalled at a bend" vs.

- Torus convexity/orientation and the `LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX=8` threshold may

- **A scouting shortcut** — try the *cheap* path first: the existing `fit_torus`/`ToroidalSurface` may fit pipe_elbow's clean bend once a few bend rows are claimed; only build the full `k=1/R` regularized model if strips defeat it.
- **Reuse** — the `JetField` medial points already give the spine seed (sidestepping the medial-axis fitter's `|n₁·n₂|>0.95` reject that strips trip).
- Acceptance models, determinism rules, debug tooling (`BREPPER_DEBUG_COMPETE`), model-reduction at commit, and the known challenges to leave for the debug session.

**MEMORY.md** now points to both the updated project state (steps 1–4 landed) and the kickoff, with the ⛔ directive flagged in the one-liner so it's visible at session start.

Both commits are in (`6f898d3` steps 1–3, `97bbda8` step 4), the tree is clean, and this is a tidy stopping point.
